 cannot help but observe the underlying sense of balance
 in these arrangements. Thus, we have three Ta Sin suras
 which were revealed consecutively, and recorded con-
 secutively. These three suras are Al-Shu‘ara’, Al-Naml,
 and Al-Qasas. And we have three Alif Lam Ra suras
 which were revealed consecutively, and recorded con-
 secutively. These suras are Minus, Hid, and Yusuf. These
 six suras which have in common the characteristic of
 being revealed and recorded consecutively are separated
 from each other by one sura, namely, Al-Isra’. And it
 is the only sura which shows a higher frequency of Ta
 Sin over the preceeding group, and also a higher overall
 frequency of Alif Lam R4 over the succeeding suras. Can
 these factual observations be mere coincidence? The data
 herein deems a coincidence highly improbable. In-
 increasingly, we are SEEING that every single word, indeed
 every single letter in the Qur’an is carefully calculated.

9. THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS Alif Lam Mim (A.L.M.):
 These three letters occur as initials in six suras. Four
 of these suras are Meccan and two are Medinan. The
 four Meccan suras are Al-‘Ankabit (The Spider), Al-
 Rim (The Romans) Luqman (Prophet Luqman), and
 Al-Sajda (Prostration). The two Medinan suras are
 Al-Baqara (The Heifer) and Ali-‘Imran (The ‘Imran
 Family). The computer printouts of the percentage
 values, the absolute frequency values, and the average
 per verse values of Alif Lam Mim are shown in Tables
 49, 50 and 51, respectively. When we look at the per-
 centage list, we note that the six Alif Lam Mim suras
 are scattered among the qur'an 114 suras. Some kind
 of combining and averaging of the Alif Lam Mim sis-
 ter-suras is clearly required. The average percentage
 value of Alif Lam Mim in the six suras is 38.049. As
 shown in Table 52, 38 suras in the Qur’an contain a
 higher percentage of Alif Lam Mim than the average
 percentage in the six Alif Lam Mim suras. To compute
 the overall frequency value of Alif Lam Mim in these
 38 suras, the magnitudes of difference above and or below
 the average of the six suras were calculated. The results
 are shown in Table 52. From this table we note that
 only two Medinan suras exhibit a higher frequency of
 Alif Lam Mim than the united six suras. The observation
 here is that the average overall frequency of the six Alif
 Lam Mim suras is SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN
 SURAS, and not to all the Medinan suras. This ob-
 observation leads to the logical step of checking the 4
 138

===== PDF PAGE 153 / PRINTED PAGE 139 =====
Meccan suras by themselves, and the 2 Medinan suras
 by themselves. The results for the united and averaged
 4 Meccan suras are shown in Table 53. From this table
 we note that all the suras which contain a higher fre-
 quincy of Alif Lam Mim than the Meccan Alif Lam
 Mim suras are Medinan suras. These are sura Al-Hadid
 (Iron), Al-Mumtahina (The Test), Al-Nisa’ (Women),
 Al-Baqara (The Heifer), Ali-‘Imran (The ‘Imran Family),
 Al-Mujadala (The Argument), Al-Ahzab (The Allies),
 Al-Ra’ad (Thunder), Muhammad, and Al-Jumu‘a
 (Friday). All these are Medinan suras (see Tables 2 and
 3). The only other suras which show a plus sign in the
 overall frequency value of Alif Lam Mim (Table 53),
 are Ibrahim (Abraham) and Minus (Jonah). These two
 suras, however, cannot be included in this particular
 comparison, since they both belong to the Alif Lam Ra
 group of suras. They are themselves initialed with Alif
 and Lam, in common with the Alif Lam Mim suras
 being discussed here. Therefore, the mathematical ob-
 observation pertaining to the Alif Lam Mim suras is that
 the averaged four Meccan suras are SUPERIOR TO
 ALL MECCAN SURAS IN THE OVERALL FRE-
 QUINCY OF ALIF LAM MIM. We note here that
 the 4 Meccan suras bearing the Qur’anic initials Alif
 Lam Mim had to be treated as if they were one continous
 sura. Their frequency values of Alif Lam Mim were
 ged. We note also that these
 grouped together and avera !
 Qur’an consecutively
 four suras are recorded in the
 aled in consecutive
 (29-32), though they were not reve
 139

===== PDF PAGE 154 / PRINTED PAGE 140 =====
order (see Tables 2 and 3). We shall observe the same
 phenomenon with the two Alif Lam Mim suras which
 were revealed in Medina, namely, Al-Baqara (The
 Heifer) and Ali-‘Imran (The ‘Imran Family). This is an
 observation of extreme importance and will be discussed
 in more detail later.
 The average percentage frequency of Alif Lam Mim
 in the two Medinan suras (Al-Baqara and Ali-‘Imran)
 is 38.556. When we insert this value in the proper place
 in Table 49, we find that 30 suras contain a higher
 percentage of Alif Lam Mim than the two united suras.
 However, computation of the overall frequency values,
 as shown in Table 54, reveals that the two Alif Lam
 Mim suras are actually SUPERIOR TO ALL SURAS
 OF THE QUR’AN.
 It should be noted that if we treat these two suras
 separately, we find that the superiority of sura Ali-‘Imran
 is surpassed by sura Al-Ma-eda (The Table). Thus, su-
 priority requires that the two Medinan Alif Lam Mim
 suras should be treated as if thev were one continuous
 sura. As mentioned above, these iwo suras are record:
 in the Qur’an consecutively (2 and 3), but they were
 not revealed in consecutive order (87 and 89). The
 Qur’anic initials Alif Lam Mim, therefore, indicate to
 us that the Alif Lam Mim suras which are recorded in
 the Qur’an in consecutive order must be treated as if
 they were one continuous sura, regardless of their
 chronological revelation sequence. These Qur’anic ini-
 trials, therefore, confirm the recorded sequence of suras
 140

===== PDF PAGE 155 / PRINTED PAGE 141 =====
as we know it today. The importance of this observation
 will become obvious when we study the Alif Lam Ra
 suras. We shall see that while the Alif Lam Mim group
 confirms the recorded sequence of Qur’an, the Alif Lam
 Ra group confirms the revelation sequence.

10. THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS Alif Lam Ra (A.L.R.):
 These letters occur in five suras, Anus (Jonah), Hid
 (Prophet Hid), Yusuf (Joseph), Ibrahim (Abraham), and
 Al-Hijr (The Hijr Valley). The recorded numbers of these
 suras are 10, 11, 12, 14, and 15, respectively. Their
 numbers in the chronological sequence of revelation are
 51, 52, 53, 72, and 54, respectively. In the recorded
 sequence of Qur’an, we note that the sequence is in-
 interrupted by sura Al-Ra‘ad (Number 13). And, in the
 chronological sequence of revelation, the order is inter-
 erupted by sura Ibrahim (number 72).
 The percentage values of Alif Lam Ra, their absolute
 frequency values, and their average occurrence per verse
 throughout the Qur’an are shown in Tables 55, 56, and
 57, respectively. When we look at the percentage list
 (Table 55), we note that the five Alif Lam Ra suras
 are scattered throughout the list. Obviously, they cannot
 exhibit mathematical superiority with regard to the
 frequency of Alif Lam Ra on individual basis. Some kind
 of averaging would be required in order to see consistent.
 meaningful observations. Thus, the following combina-
 tions were studied in detail.
 1. All five Alif Lam Ra suras were combined and
 averaged. i.e., treated as if they were one continuous
 sura.
 2. The four Alif Lam Ra suras which were revealed
 consecutively were combined and averaged. These
 suras are Anus (Jonah), Hid, Yusuf (Joseph), and
 Al-Hijr (The Hijr Valley).
 148

===== PDF PAGE 163 / PRINTED PAGE 149 =====
3. Sura Ibrahim (Abraham) which was revealed sep-
 rarely from the other Alif Lam Ra suras was
 studied by itself.
 4. The Alif Lam Ra suras which are recorded con-
 secutively in the Qur’an were combined together
 and averaged. These included two groups. One
 group consisted of suras Anus, Hid, and Yusuf,
 whose recorded numbers are 10, 11, and 12. The
 other group consisted of suras Ibrahim and Al-Hijr,
 whose recorded numbers are 14 and 15.
 The overall frequency values of the suras which
 contained higher percentages of Alif Lam Ra were
 computed as previously detailed, and compared with the
 various averages of Alif Lam Ra suras. Due to the vast
 number of calculations and tabulations involved, only
 the final observations are reported here. The following
 mathematical phenomena were observed:
 1. When all five Alif Lam R4 suras were treated as
 if they were one continuous sura, their average was
 found SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN SURAS,
 EXCEPT AL-ISRA’ (The Night Journey, no. 17).
 . The same conclusion was obtained when the four
 consecutively-revealed suras were combined. This
 meant the exclusion of sura Ibrahim, which was
 revealed some 18 suras after its 4 sister suras.
 3. When sura Ibrahim was considered by itself, it was
 also found SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN
 SURAS EXCEPT SURA AL-ISRA’ (no. 17). with
 regard to the overall frequency of Alif Lam Ra.
 149

===== PDF PAGE 164 / PRINTED PAGE 150 =====
4. When the three suras revealed consecutively, and
 also recorded consecutively, were combined, their
 average overall frequency of Alif Lam Ra was found
 SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN SURAS
 WITHOUT EXCEPTION.
 5. When the two suras recorded consecutively, but not
 revealed consecutively (Ibrahim and Al-Hijr) were
 combined, their average overall frequency of Alif
 Lam Ra was found SUPERIOR TO ALL MEC-
 CAN SURAS EXCEPT SURA AL-ISRA’.
 6. When sura Al-Hijr was studied by itself, it showed
 no superiority in the overall frequency of Alif Lam
 Ra.
 These observations need no further explanation. The
 reader is reminded that all Alif Lam Ra suras are
 Meccan. The special position of Al-Isra’ has been ex-
 planned on page 125. Here in the Alif Lam Ra group.
 sura Al-Isra’ seems to function as a distinguishing factor
 between the suras which were revealed and also recorded
 consecutively (observation #4), and the suras which were
 recorded consecutively but revealed separately (observa-
 tion no. 1, 2, 3, & 5).
 The most important observation here is that the Alif
 Lam Ra suras must be combined according to their
 revelation sequence, regardless of their recorded se-
 quence. We have seen that the Alif Lam Mim suras were
 required to joint each other according to their recorded
 sequence, regardless of their revelation order. This is
 clarified in Table 58. Therefore, the Qur’anic initials Alif
 150

===== PDF PAGE 165 / PRINTED PAGE 151 =====
Lam Mim inform us that the recorded sequence of the
 Qur’an is the correct, divinely inspired recording se-
 quence. These three letters occur in two Medinan suras
 which are recorded consecutively, though revealed sep-
 rarely, and in four Meccan suras which are recorded
 consecutively, though revealed separately (see Table 58).
 The Medinan group is separated from the Meccan group,
 and we saw that mathematical superiority is observed
 only when these two groups are separated, with each
 group united and averaged.
 On the other hand, the function of the initials Alif
 Lam Ra is to inform us that the revelation sequence
 of the Qur’an is the correct, divinely inspired, revelation
 sequence. We have seen that sura Al-Hijr by itself exhibits
 no mathematical superiority in the overall frequency of
 Alif Lam Ra. It had to be combined with its sister suras
 which preceded it directly in the chronological sequence.
 As shown in Table 58, in the recorded sequence of
 Qur’an, sura Al-Hijr (no. 15) is separated from its sister
 suras by sura Al-Ra’ad (no. 13), and sura Ibrahim (no.
 14).
 151

===== PDF PAGE 166 / PRINTED PAGE 152 =====

11. THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS Alif Lam Mim Sad
 (A.L.M.S.):
 This set of Qur’anic initials occurs in one sura, namely
 Al-A‘raf (The Purgatory). This sura ranks 7 in the re-
 corded sequence, and 39 in the revelation sequence of
 Qur’an. The percentage values of Alif Lam Mim Sad,
 the absolute frequency values, and the average per verse
 values are shown in Tables 59, 60, and 61, respective-
 ly. Computation of the overall frequency of Alif Lam
 Mim Sad was carried out for all the suras which contain
 a higher percentage than sura Al-A‘raf. The results are
 shown in Table 62. Although a relatively large number
 of suras do have a higher percentage, only four suras
 are actually higher than sura Al-A‘raf in the overall
 frequency of Alif Lam Mim Sad. These four suras are
 Al-Nisa’ (Women), Al-Baqara (The Heifer), Ali-‘Imran
 (The ‘Imran Family), and Al- Ma-eda (The Table). All
 these are Medinan suras. It appears, therefore, that sura
 Al-A‘raf is mathematically SUPERIOR TO ALL THE
 MECCAN SURAS in the overall frequency of Alif Lam
 Mim Sad. We note that sura Al-A‘raf is a Meccan sura
 which occurs in a predominately Medinan section of the
 Qur’an. We also note that this Alif Lam Mim Sad sura
 is a loner with regard to this particular set of initials.
 Thus, just as the sura was not combined and averaged
 with any other sura, no combining or averaging of any
 suras was required to see the superiority of Al-A‘raf over
 all the Meccan suras.

12. THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS Alif Lam Mim Ra
 (A.L.M.R.):
 This set of initials occurs in one sura, namely, Al-Ra‘d
 (Thunder), which ranks 13 in the recorded sequence and
 96 in the revelation sequence of Qur’an. The percent
 occurrence of these letters, their absolute frequency, and
 their average occurrence per verse throughout the Qur’an
 are shown in Tables 63, 64, and 65, respectively. When
 we look at the percentage figures (Table 63), we note
 that sura Al-Ra‘d (Thunder) has the high rank of 84
 among the qur'an 114 suras. However, when we take
 a closer look at the suras which contain a higher per-
 centage of Alif Lam Mim Ra, we observe that all these
 suras are in fact lower in the overall frequency values.
 Using all three values to calculate the magnitudes of
 difference above and/or below sura Al-Ra‘d, we find that
 ALL the suras with a higher percentage bear a negative
 sign in front of the net difference as compared to Al-Ra‘d
 (see Table 66).
 This observation in effect tells us that sura Al-Ra‘d
 is SUPERIOR TO ALL THE SURAS WITHOUT
 EXCEPTION in the overall frequency of Alif Lam Mim
 Ra. We note that this set of initials occurs in a single
 sura, where no combining/averaging is required. Con-
 sequently, no combining/averaging of any suras was
 required to see the mathematical superiority of sura
 Al-Ra‘d. The reader will note that this has been a
 consistent observation throughout the study. Whenever
 the initials occurred in a number of suras, it was required
 161

===== PDF PAGE 176 / PRINTED PAGE 162 =====
to combine/average all the suras with identical initials.
 On the other hand, when combining/averaging was not
 required for the initialed sura, no combining/averaging
 was required anywhere else.
 Another worthwhile observation is that the initialed
 Meccan suras were always superior to ALL Meccan suras,
 while the initialed Medinan suras were found superior
 to ALL the suras, Meccan and Medinan. This was
 observed here in sura Al-Ra‘d with regard to Alif Lam
 Mim Ra, and also in the two Medinan suras Al-Baqara
 and Ali-‘Imran when we discussed the Alif Lam Mim
 initials. The reader recalls that Alif Lam Mim Sad which
 occur in the Meccan sura Al-A‘raf were superior in that
 sura over ALL the Meccan suras, but not all the Medinan
 suras. The same observation of course holds for the seven
 Meccan Ha Mim suras, the Meccan Ta HA sura, and
 the Meccan Alif Lam Mim suras. The fact that the
 superiority of the initialed Meccan suras was limited to
 the Meccan suras confirms for us the locations of rev-
 elation. And the fact that the mathematical superiority
 of all the initialed Medinan suras covers ALL suras of
 the Qur’an without exception, informs us that the details
 of the divine commandments which are provided in the
 Medinan suras consummate, complement, and perfect
 the initial commandments in the Meccan suras.

13. THE QURVANIC INITIALS Kaf Ha Ya ‘Ayn Sad
 (KHY‘AS):
 This set of initials occurs in one Meccan sura, namely,
 Maryam (Mary). It ranks 19 in the recorded sequence;
 44 in the revelation sequence of Qur’an. There is only
 one sura which bears one of the initials in common with
 sura Maryam, and which occurs in consecutive order
 after Maryam both in the recording and the‘revelation
 sequences. This one sura is Ta Ha, which ranks 20 in
 the recorded sequence; 45 in the revelation sequence.
 The percentage occurrence values of Kaf Ha Ya ‘Ayn
 Sad in each sura, the absolute frequency of occurrence
 values, and the average per verse values are shown in
 Tables 67, 68, and 69, respectively. When we look at
 the percentage list, we note that sura Maryam does have
 a high percentage of the letters Kaf Ha Ya ‘Ayn Sad.
 Only 34 suras out of the qur'an 114 contain a higher
 percentage of these initials. However, we note that if
 we take into consideration the letter Ha which occurs
 consecutively in suras Maryam and Ta HA, we note that
 only 20 suras contain a higher percentage of Kaf Ha
 Ya ‘Ayn Sad. By combining and averaging the letter
 Ha only of suras Maryam and Ta Ha, then using this
 average percentage of Ha to calculate the Kaf Ha Ya
 ‘Ayn Sad percentage, the resulting value is 21.005. When
 we insert this value in its proper place in the percentage
 table, we see that only 20 suras have a higher percentage
 value. Thus, the overall frequency of Kaf Ha Ya ‘Ayn
 Sad was calculated for each of the 34 suras which are
 167

===== PDF PAGE 182 / PRINTED PAGE 168 =====
higher before considering the letter Ha of sura Ta Ha
 and also for the 20 suras which are higher after con-
 sidering the letter Ha from sura Ta Ha. The results are
 shown in Tables 70 and 71, respectively. We note from
 Table 70 that nine suras have higher overall frequencies
 of Kaf Ha Ya ‘Ayn Sad than sura Maryam. These suras
 are Yusuf (Joseph), Al-Zumur (The Throngs), Al-Anfal
 (The Spoils), Al-Qasas (History), Al-Nuar (The Light).
 Al-Hajj (Pilgrimage), Al-Shira (Consensus), Ta Ha
 (T.H.), and Muhammad. Since these include Meccan
 as well as Medinan suras, we conclude that sura Maryam
 lacks the overall superiority when considered by itself.
 It appears therefore, that we are unable to ignore the
 fact that the letter Ha was revealed and recorded con-
 secutively in the two suras Maryam and Ta Ha. The
 initial Ha even occupies the same place in both suras.
 namely, the second spot. Very clearly, we are told here
 that the placement of the letter Ha in these two suras
 was not accidental. Once again we are shown that each
 alphabet in the Qur’an was carefully calculated.
 By looking at Table 71, we note right away that only
 six suras bear the plus sign, ie., they are higher than
 sura Maryam in the overall frequency of KHY‘AS. These
 six suras are Al-Qasas, (History), Al-Nir (The Light.
 Al-Hajj (Pilgrimage), Al-Shara (Consensus), Ta Ha
 (T.H.), and Al-Fat-h (Victory). Thus, each contains a
 higher overall frequency of the initials Kaf Ha Ya ‘Ayn
 Sad. And we note that three of these suras are Meccan.
 the other three are Medinan. But we also note that all
 168

===== PDF PAGE 183 / PRINTED PAGE 169 =====
three Meccan suras are initialed suras.
 Sura Al-Qasas (History) is initialed with Ta Sin Mim
 and should be combined/averaged with its sister sura.
 This takes it out of this comparison. Sura Al-Shira
 (Consensus) belongs to the Ha Mim group and should
 be combined/averaged with its six sister suras. The re-
 maining Meccan sura is Ta Ha which of course bears
 the letter Ha in common with sura Maryam. Thus, we
 see here again that when combining/averaging is re-
 quired of the initialed suras, the same combining/
 averaging is required of the suras being compared.
 The observation to be recorded here is that, when the
 letter Ha of both Maryam and Ta Ha is taken into
 consideration, sura Maryam is SUPERIOR TO ALL
 MECCAN SURAS in the overall frequency of Kaf Ha
 Ya ‘Ayn Sad. The reader is reminded that both suras
 Maryam and Ta Ha are Meccan. This observation is
 consistent with the other initialed Meccan suras as
 discussed on page 162; a fact that confirms the location
 of revelation of the Qur’anic chapters.
 169

===== PDF PAGE 184 / PRINTED PAGE 170 =====

14. THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS Ha Mim ‘Ayn Sin Qaf
 (H.M. ‘A.S.Q.):
 This set of initials occurs in one sura, namely, Al-Shira
 (Consensus). It ranks 42 in the recorded sequence; 62
 in the revelation sequence of the Qur’an. The first two
 letters, Ha Mim, form an independent verse. In that
 respect, sura Al-Shira belongs to the Ha Mim group
 of seven suras. The three letters ‘Ayn Sin Qaf constitute
 verse 2 of sura Al-Shira. The percentage occurrence of
 these initials, their absolute frequency in each sura, and
 their average occurrence in each verse of each sura are
 listed in Tables 72, 73, and 74, respectively. The reader
 by now is familiar with the types of computations and
 the bases for finding out the overall frequency value for
 a given set of Qur’anic initials. When these computations
 were carried out for the five letters Ha Mim ‘Ayn Sin
 Qaf, it was observed that sura Al-Shira exhibits no
 mathematical superiority. As a matter of fact, 18 suras
 were found superior to sura Al-Shira in the overall
 frequency of Ha Mim ‘Ayn Sin Qaf. Fourteen of these
 suras were Meccan, the other 4 Medinan. Of the 14
 superior Meccan suras, 8 were initialed suras, and 6 were
 regular, non-initialed suras. Therefore, when considered
 by itself, sura Al-Shira cannot claim any mathematical
 superiority with regard to its own initials. It appears,
 therefore, that we cannot ignore the sister suras of
 Al-Shara (the other 6 Ha Mim suras).
 175

===== PDF PAGE 190 / PRINTED PAGE 176 =====
This lack of mathematical superiority was also observed
 when sura Al-Shira was considered by itself with regard
 to the initials Ha Mim, and also with regard to the initials
 ‘Ayn Sin Qaf. When the average Ha Mim of the seven
 Ha Mim suras was added to the ‘Ayn Sin Qéaf values
 of sura Al-Shara, the computer data again showed lack
 of superiority. Finally, the values of ‘Ayn Sin Qaf in
 the seven Ha Mim suras were combined together and
 averaged in the usual manner. The average of ‘Ayn Sin
 Qaf was then added to the one Ha Mim of sura Al-Shara
 to represent the Qur’anic initials Ha Mim‘Ayn Sin Qaf.
 This is the ONLY combination where mathematical
 superiority was observed. Results of computing the
 overall frequency values are shown in Table 75, and the
 attempted combinations, with their results, are -shown
 in Table 76. From Table 75, we can see that only 3
 suras have the plus sign, indicating their superiority over
 the Ha Mim-‘Ayn Sin Qaf average of the seven suras.
 These 3 superior suras are Hid (Prophet Hid), Al-Ram
 (The Romans), and Al-Fat-h (Victory). Strikingly, the
 only other two sets of Qur’anic initials which occur in
 consecutive suras, namely, Alif Lam Ra and Alif Lam
 Mim, are represented here. Thus, sura Hid represents
 the Alif Lam Ra group, and sura Al-Rim represents
 the Alif Lam Mim group. After combining these two
 suras with their respective sister suras, only one sura is
 left which exhibits superiority over the Ha Mim group
 with regard to the overall frequency of Ha Mim-‘Ayn
 Sin Qaf. This one sura, Al-Fat-h, is a Medinan sura.
 176

===== PDF PAGE 191 / PRINTED PAGE 177 =====
Therefore, when the seven Ha Mim suras are taken into
 consideration with regard to the ‘Ayn Sin Qéaf initials,
 the resulting Ha Mim-‘Ayn Sin Qaf in them is SUPE-
 RIOR TO ALL MECCAN SURAS. The reader is re-
 minded here that this is the same conclusion observed
 for the initials Ha Mim in the seven Ha Mim suras
 (page 114).
 Similarly, if we disregard the average per verse values,
 and rely only on the percentage values and absolute
 frequency values in our computations, we find that sura
 Al-Shira is SUPERIOR TO ALL SURAS OF THE
 QUR’AN WITHOUT EXCEPTION with regard to the
 initials HA Mim-‘Ayn Sin Qaf (of course the ‘Ayn Sin
 Qaf of the sister Ha Mim suras must be considered as
 pointed out above). This observation, too, was noted for
 the initials Ha Mim (page 114).

Chapter 7
 SUMMARY AND CONCLUSION
 The Qur’anic initials and the major observations
 pertaining to them are all listed in Table 77. By glancing
 through this table, the reader notes that each one of
 the Qur’anic initials has its own characteristics. Each
 one serves to reassure man with regard to specific aspects
 of the Qur’an (such as the sequence of recording, the
 sequence of revelation, the length of the verses, the length
 of the suras, the location of revelation, etc.). Thus, all
 aspects of the Qur’an are proven and confirmed. These
 aspects include:
 1. The Qur’an is the word of God:
 The Quranic initials as a whole have shown us that
 every word, indeed every letter, in the Qur’an was
 carefully calculated. The Qur’an itself states this fact
 very clearly in the first verse of sura Hid, which in-
 accidentally includes the Qur’anic initials Alif Lam Ra.
 Placement of the Quranic initials in their specific lo-
 cations proves the existence of advance knowledge of the
 distribution pattern of the alphabet throughout the
 Qur’an. No one can claim that such advance knowledge
 is attainable by man; any man.
 To translate this into a physical, tangible evidence,
 the computer was asked to calculate the number of
 manipulations one should master in order to write a
 mathematically-controlled book such as the Qur’an. The
 composition of Qur’an involves 114 chapters where 14
 alphabet letters were distributed according to specific
 122

===== PDF PAGE 198 / PRINTED PAGE 184 =====
combinations. According to the well-known mathemat-
 ical formula, the number of manipulations involved in
 this case equals 11414. The computer’s answer was that
 11414 = 6.26 x 1026 is the number of manipulations
 that must be controlled in order to write a book like
 the Qur’an. This value, 626,000,000,000,000,000,000,
 000,000 (626 septillions) is certainly beyond the capacity
 of any creature, including today’s most sophisticated
 computers. When we look at this number, we can readily
 appreciate the divine statement in sura Al-Isra’ (The
 Night Journey), verse 88 (Say, if all the humans and
 all the Jinns banded together in order to produce a
 like this, they will surely fail ١
 Furthermore, this is the number of controlled manipu-
 lations for a specific distribution pattern of the alphabet,
 without placing them in useful sentences. To place these
 mathematically distributed alphabetic letters in useful
 sentences is another complete job. This fact, and the full
 awareness of the two requirements, is indicated at the
 beginning of sura Hid. The first verse in this sura says,
 “Alif Lam Ra. This is a book whose verses have been
 perfectly designed THEN ELUCIDATED. It comes from
 a Wise, Omniscient (God).”
 Thus, this verse tells us of the two jobs required; the
 job of distributing the alphabet in a perfectly controlled
 and designed pattern, AND the job of making from these
 letters meaningful sentences. The reader can easily vi-
 realize the awesomeness of both jobs.

 puts in our hands physical, examinable evidence that
 Moses did indeed split the sea with his staff. Though
 we never saw the staff of Moses turn into a snake, we
 now have physical proof that such a miracle took place
 indeed. Since every word in the Qur’an is mathematically
 proven to have come from God, we now know for sure
 that Jesus Christ did indeed revive the dead. In summary,
 by proving that the Qur’an is God’s undisputable rev-
 elation, all the Qur’anic miracles of previous prophets
 and messengers are equally proven.

 5. The particular way of spelling certain words in the Qur’an
 is divinely dictated:
 A well known fact is that the Qur’anic Arabic is
 unique. It is not identical to conventional Arabic. For
 example the word “salat” is written as “salawah” ة‎
 م‎
 instead of صلاة‎ , the word “zakat” is written as
 “Zakawah” Pt instead of 8&6) , the word
 “hayat (life)” is written as “Hayawah ةريح‎ ” instead
 of >. Many other words are written in a unique
 way in the Qur’an, such as إمرات‎ بصلة
 etc. Needless to say, changing the Qur’anic Arabic intc
 conventional Arabic will upset all the mathematical
 characteristics of the qur'an alphabet, and will render
 the Qur’anic initials totally meaningless and insignia
 cant. Only recently, there have been demands to change
 the qur'an Arabic into conventional Arabic in order
 to “make the Qur’an easier.” Such demands were quite
 frequent in the past five years, especially in Egypt. The
 findings of this study, therefore, are quite timely. They
 inform us that the specific way of writing the Qur’an
 should never be altered in any way.
 6. The qur'an specific way of dividing each sura into verses
 ts divinely prescribed:
 The lengths of verses as they came to us must never
 be tampered with. A good example should be pointed
 out here. When ‘Abdullah Yusuf ‘Ali translated the

meanings of the Qur’an, he arbitrarily (and I am sure
 with good and sincere intentions) changed the lengths
 of certain verses “because they sounded better to him.”
 An example of this is verse #3 of sura #3 (Ali-‘Imran).
 The verse came to us with the ending at the word
 “Al-Injil.” But ‘Abdullah Yusuf ‘Ali changed the position
 of ending the verse to the word “Al-Furqan” of verse
 #4. In view of this study, this type of alteration is not
 permissible. As we have seen from the Qur’anic initials
 Ha Mim and Ha Mim-‘Ayn Sin Qaf, the average per
 verse value is a highly significant property throughout
 the Qur’an. When the average per verse values were
 considered, the suras containing these initials were su-
 period to all the Meccan suras, and not to all the Medinan
 suras. And when these values were disregarded, the su-
 priority of these suras covered all suras of the Qur’an
 without exception.
 7. The opening statement “Bismillahi-r-Rahmani-r-Rahim (In

 i - This opening statement is an independent verse in

It must be repeated here that this study merely
 scratches the surface; unveils the tip of a huge iceberg.
 Undoubtedly, many Muslims who are better versed in
 mathematics will deduce many more conclusions from
 the data presented here. During the course of this work
 many side phenomena were observed. Pursuing any one
 of those phenomena would have distracted the worker
 from any specific path, and would easily take the life
 time of many workers to reach a full and complete
 understanding. It is for that reason that all the raw data
 are included in this book.
 Finally, one cannot help but observe that exactly 14
 centuries have passed before the uncovering of the
 mathematical properties of these 14 sets of Qur’anic
 initials, which are composed of 14 letters. This year, 1393
 AH-1973 AD, marks 14 lunar centuries from the time
 of the first revelation of Qur’an, and 14 solar centuries
 from the time of Muhammad’s birth. There is even a
 special significance of the number 14 in the older
 scriptures. In the gospel of Matthew, Chapter 1 verse
 17, it says, “Thus, the total number of generations is:
 from Abraham to David, 14 generations; from David
 to the Babylonian captivity, 14 generations; from the
 Babylonian captivity to the Messiah, 14 generations.”
 A natural question then would be, “Why did God
 Almighty choose to conceal the significance of the 14
 Qur’anic initials for 14 centuries?” In the authors
 humble opinion, this deliberate concealment is correlated
 to the manner in which the Qur’an was revealed. Thus.
 190

===== PDF PAGE 205 / PRINTED PAGE 191 =====
we have here a book which was written over a period
 of 23 years. Its individual verses came down scattered,
 ,عا‎ widely separated from each other both in time and
 place. Some Meccan suras include Medinan verses, and
 some Medinan suras include Meccan verses. The se-
 quence of recording the Qur’anic chapters does not follow
 any particular system. The recorded sequence is totally
 unrelated to the chronological sequence of revelation.
 This seemingly confused manner has, in turn, confused
 the enemies of the Qur’an. When the prejudiced critics
 of the Qur’an found no scientific contradictions in the
 Qur’an, they rejected the Qur’an as the word of God
 on the basis that “it was written haphazardly.” When
 they failed to detect any contradictions in the Qur’an,
 they attacked the qur'an method of revelation and
 recording. During the past 14 centuries, the enemies of
 Islam settled for the conclusion that a “randomly
 written” book like that could not be the word of God.
 Now, after those critics settled for this particular con-
 cousin, people can find out for themselves, through this
 book, that every word in the Qur’an, indeed every letter,
 was carefully calculated and perfectly designed. The
 obvious question to such critics is “Now, what do you
 have to say? On what basis can you now reject the Qur’an
 as God’s message to humanity?”

who revealed the Qur’an. For example, the Qur’anic
 initials Alif Lam Mim of sura Al-Baqgara are followed
 by the statement, “This book cannot be doubted.” The
 Alif Lam Mim in the next sura, Ali-‘Imran, are followed
 by the statement, “Allah, the one and only God; the
 Living; the Eternal God, is the One who revealed this
 book to you (O Muhammad).” The next three suras
 Al-Nisa’, Al-Ma-eda, and Al-An‘am, do not have any
 Qur’anic initials. These three suras lack the opening
 statement about the revelation of Qur’an from God. Then
 comes sura Al-A‘raf. This sura does have Qur’anic
 initials. The first two verses of this sura say, “A.L.M.S.
 This book is being revealed to you (O Muhammad) and
 you shall have no doubts about it. You shall warn
 therewith. It is a message to the believers.” Thus
 throughout the Qur’an, we observe this kind of statement
 immediately following each one of the Quranic initials.
 The only exceptions to this rule occur in suras Al:
 ‘Ankabit (no. 29) and Al-Rim (no. 30). However, as
 the reader recalls, these two suras belong to the Meccan
 group of suras which are initialed with Alif Lam Mim.
 The first revealed sura in this group is Luqman. Sura
 Luqman starts with the Qur’anic initials Alif Lam Mim,
 followed by the characteristic statement about the
 qur'an perfect composition.
 Finally, the reader can easily appreciate the miracle
 of the Qur’an by reflecting on the following simplified
 example:
 Suppose you are asked to write a book of 10 unequal
 192

===== PDF PAGE 207 / PRINTED PAGE 193 =====
chapters, on condition that the first chapter must contain
 the highest overall frequency of the letter A. You will
 probably carry out this task without much effort. All
 you will have to do is to make use of the letter A more
 often in the first chapter than any other chapter. Now,
 let us add one more condition. In addition to the high
 relative frequency of the letter A in the first chapter,
 you are required to show the highest relative frequency
 of the letters B and C in chapter 2. You can easily see
 that the task of writing the book would be more difficult.
 You may still be able to write the book, but now you
 have to control the distribution of the three letters A,
 B, and C. You have to choose words containing the letter
 A more frequently as you compose chapter 1, and you
 have to utilize words containing the letters B and C more
 frequently as you compose chapter 2. Now let us add
 the condition that the first three chapters must contain
 a higher percentage of the letter D than chapter 4, and
 the last three chapters must contain a lower percentage
 of D than chapter 7. You can see that the task is already
 becoming impossible. You have to choose certain words,
 utilizing certain alphabetic letters, in order to satisfy the
 requirements mentioned. You have to put more A’s in
 the first chapter, more B’s and C’s in the second chapter,
 and you have to keep track of the frequency and pro-
 portions of the letter D throughout the book. And let
 us not forget that you have to put those mathematically
 controlled words in useful, comprehensible, sentences.
 Not only meaningful sentences, but also sentences which
 193

===== PDF PAGE 208 / PRINTED PAGE 194 =====
are linguistically and grammatically perfect.
 This example illustrates for us that even after knowing
 the mathematical rules of composing the Qur’an, no
 human being in the past, present, or future can compose
 a similar work. We have been told by the computer that
 one has to master 626,000,000,000,000,000,000,000,000
 combinations of alphabetic letters in order to produce
 the mathematical distribution of the qur'an alphabet.
 Composing useful sentences from these letters is an ad-
 national task.
 It is, therefore, no wonder that God tells us in His
 message:

 it humbled and cracked from the glory of God (59:21)
 And we now know for sure that:

 in order to produce a Qur’an like this, they will surely
 fail, no matter how much assistance they may lend one
 another. (17:88).

Addendum
 An investigation into the significance of the Qur’anic
 initials would be incomplete without referring to what
 the Prophet Muhammad said about them. The following
 account is well documented in the reliable sources of
 Hadith and Qur’anic studies, including Al-Baydawy’s
 commentary and Al-Suyity’s classic reference, “Al-Itqan
 Fi ‘Ulam Al-Qur’an.” A group of Jewish scripturists were
 discussing the Qur’an with the Prophet. When he recited
 the first few verses of sura Al-Baqara (The Heifer) to
 them, they said, “How do you expect us to believe in
 a religion which is supposed to survive 71 years only?”
 This was an obvious reference to the numerical value
 they assigned to the Qur’anic initials Alif Lam Mim
 (ALM) which make up the first verse of sura Al-Baqara.
 The numerical value of the letter A, according to the
 ancient science of gametria, is 1, the numerical value
 of the letter L is 30, and the value of the letter M is
 40. When added up, the numerical value of ALM is
 71. The significant incident in that confrontation be-
 tween the Prophet and the Jewish scripturists, is that
 he did not object to their method of assigning numerical
 values to ALM, and using the product as a measure
 of Islam’s life-time. The Prophet’s answer was, “But you
 forget that there is also ALMS, ALR, ALMR.” The lack
 of objection on the part of the Prophet to assigning
 numerical values to the Qur’anic initials is quite 8
 Qur’anic
 nificant. Thus, the numerical values of all the :
 د:‎ the
 initials were calculated. The results are shown 2
 195

===== PDF PAGE 210 / PRINTED PAGE 196 =====
Table below. The grand total of these calculations is
 4949. If the inference is indeed correct, from the con-
 rotation incident between the Prophet and the Jewish
 scripturists, this number (4949 years) may indicate the
 life-time of Islam.
